# Monster duplicate-name review — 22 October 2026

Read-only review of the current 334-entry standard index and 680 signed-in imported monsters against the previously resolved built-in 5etools catalog. Rechecked the live imported names and sources; they are unchanged from the audit.

No monster was renamed, merged, deleted or imported. No builder changes were made.

## The listing model to review

Use one browse listing per creature family. Source labels such as UA, MM, VGM or MPMM belong in a version selector; spelling aliases should find that same listing. Forms, loadouts and optional traits can live inside the listing. Different encounter roles still need distinct creature identities.

The rows below overlap: one creature can have a typo, an option variant and a later renamed source version. **213 review rows does not mean 213 proven duplicates or disjoint families.**

## Strong candidates to address first

| Current names | Finding |
|---|---|
| Aarakroca / Aarakocra | Likely spelling error; the original MM candidate also matches the compared core stats. |
| Nyacaloth / Nycaloth | Likely spelling error. |
| Pteradon / Pteranodon | Likely spelling error. |
| Storm Giant Quintessant / Storm Giant Quintessent | Likely spelling error. |
| Titivllus / Titivilus | Likely spelling error. |
| Duergar Soulblades / Duergar Soulblade | Singular/plural label difference. |
| Rothe / Rothé; Deep Rothe / Deep Rothé | Accent differences. |
| Nikya / Druid of the Old Ways | Same remaining stored detail fields under the comparison described below. |
| Mizzix / Galvanic Blastseeker | Same remaining stored detail fields under that comparison. |
| Cow / Rothe | Same remaining stored detail fields in the current import; retain species/flavour as a reskin rather than asserting the source creatures are universally identical. |
| Geetra / Precognitive Mage | Stored fields match after also excluding languages. |
| Izoni / Golgari Shaman | Stored fields match after also excluding languages. |
| Judith / Rakdos Lampooner | Stored fields match after also excluding languages. |

Stored-field comparison excluded root name, key, source, disabled flag, flavour description and edit-event metadata. It normalized case, whitespace and self-name references, and sorted object keys recursively. It did not discard rule bodies. These are strong grouping candidates within the **current imports**, not a claim that every official/source edition has identical rules.

Two UA Alchemical Homunculus imports also match the stored-field comparison. Other same-name source pairs need content review.

## Vampire specifically

There is no current monster named “Vampire - UA.” The current group is:

- Vampire, Vampire Form — SRD 2014
- Vampire, Bat Form — SRD 2014
- Vampire, Mist Form — SRD 2014
- Vampire Spellcaster — Monster Manual import
- Vampire Warrior — Monster Manual import

These are good candidates for one **Vampire** browse listing with form, variant and source selections. The spellcaster and warrior have real mechanical differences; do not delete those rules. The public catalog also contains MM and XMM Vampire source versions. Its reprint metadata links the older Spellcaster/Warrior entries to the newer Vampire; that does not prove all versions have identical content.

Keep Vampire Spawn, Blood Drinker Vampire, Mind Drinker Vampire and Vampiric Mist separately identifiable. They can share a Vampire browse family, but are different creatures/roles. Future Vampire Familiar, Nightbringer or Umbral Lord entries likewise need rule review rather than a blanket name merge.

## Renamed versions and misleading matches

There are 68 reviewed source-link rows classified as renamed source versions. Examples include Goblin → Goblin Warrior, Androsphinx → Sphinx of Valor, Gynosphinx → Sphinx of Lore, Flying Sword → Animated Flying Sword, Quipper → Piranha, Abjurer → Abjurer Wizard, and Bronze Scout → Clockwork Bronze Scout. The full source-qualified list follows.

Source links sometimes suggest **replacements**, not synonyms. For example, Drow → Priest Acolyte, Duergar → Spy, Lizardfolk → Scout and Orc → Tough must not collapse those different creature identities into one. The source's reprint annotations are evidence of a relationship, not universal equivalence. [Wizards' conversion guide](https://media.dndbeyond.com/compendium-images/srd/guide/converting-to-srd-5.2.1.pdf) also distinguishes renamed content from omitted statblocks with suggested replacements.

Four named NPCs have partial-stat matches to generic MM creatures: Arrester Lavinia/Veteran, Emmara Tandris/Priest, Master Chemister Grozgrox/Mage and Teysa Karlov/Mage. Their HP, AC, CR and all six ability scores match; their full rules were not certified equivalent. Treat these as lower-confidence review candidates.

## Complete candidate list

Source abbreviations: MM = Monster Manual 2014; XMM = Monster Manual 2025; VGM = Volo's; MTF = Mordenkainen's Tome of Foes; MPMM = Monsters of the Multiverse; GGR = Ravnica; ERLW = Eberron; FRAiF = Forgotten Realms: Adventures in Faerûn; UA = Unearthed Arcana. Current full source labels are retained.

The [JSON evidence](audits/monster-name-review-2026-10-22.json) and [CSV review list](audits/monster-name-review-2026-10-22.csv) contain the same rows. Original source metadata was read from the [5etools bestiary datasets](https://5e.tools/data/bestiary/index.json).

## Limits and next decision

These are candidates for **how creatures should appear in search and browsing**. This review does not choose a global preferred edition, erase a UA entry, or turn a named character into a generic NPC automatically. Select the preferred source/version and family policy before builder work.

The scan covers current names, explicit source reprint links, named forms/options, fuzzy spelling candidates, and normalized stored-field matches. It is not an exhaustive semantic equivalence proof across every rule in 5etools or optional homebrew. Conclave Dryad Mount remains unassigned rather than being forced into a guessed match.

Artifact checks verify row counts, references and evidence coverage; application tests were unnecessary because runtime code was unchanged.
